Three causes in ai_euro.c: (1) every Europe exit set the opening west-explore goto (4,y) even mid-game — greedy ocean steps went west into a land pocket, the pathfinder fallback routed back east, net zero per turn; now an established nation's exit goto is coastal water by the passenger's colony / nearest own coastal colony. (2) Greedy-then-pathfinder each act undid itself; the stall now latches the pathfinder for that goto (`s_euro_ship_route_latch`, cleared on any goto write). (3) The thin Europe-export arm sailed with any export cargo, so a Caravel round-tripped Europe every 4 turns with 2–8 furs; it now needs ≥50 aboard (its own stock>99/leave-50 load rule). Follow-up (same day): the thin "nearest foe ship / enemy port" naval hunt and the Galleon "war transport target" were port inventions — DOS has no distant naval hunt. Ported the real thing: raw 90210-90219 sends an idle hull (or a busy one with a foreign unit adjacent) into the shared 8-direction scorer LAB_521d_4d2e, whose ship arms (open-sea step, far-roam latch raw 88632, fort/Artillery term × holds, west lean, LAB_52aa odds attack — attack-0 hulls skip foe tiles) are now in `ai_euro_20e6_wander_step` / `_ship_far_roam` / `_ship_wander_act`. Idle AI ships roam as in DOS instead of parking; warships fight what the pick puts next to them. Also guarded the opening-only "post-found cruise tip" arm (one colony, water tip) which had yanked a mid-game hull onto a land tile. 61/61, goldens byte-green, no cycles on any campaign save. |
| 450 | CLOSED | Euro ship moves are shown on their turns even with no human units or colonies anywhere near them, as long as the tile is not under fog of war. (1) `s_units_goto_last_dir` (FUN_6662_0f74 anti-backtrack shadow) was zero-initialised and read as "last step = North", and the AI ship sail loop never recorded its own `units_try_move` steps into it; a far goal whose first flood/waypoint step was South was rejected as a reversal, the greedy tier took over and ping-ponged (32,51)↔(31,50) forever. (2) The sail loop refused ANY occupied tile for both the greedy and the pathfinder step; two own ships whose routes crossed each other blocked each other indefinitely. Only a foreign occupant blocks now (own ships stack). Both ships route around the southern cape and reach the east coast within ~20 turns; 61/61 green, no cycles on campaign3 00/01/05/08. |

What the row did expose is a save-interop defect, now fixed in `col1_bridge.c`: the port wrote its Europe harbor at `228+n` and Bound at `232+n`, so a real DOS westbound stack one tick from landing (lategame `COLONY06.SAV`: Dutch hulls at 231 with counter 0, chained Artillery and goto (53,56), vs the French at 233 with counter 1) imported as a docked ship and re-exported one lane too far out. Lanes are now `236+n` harbor / `228+n` Bound (counter 0 kept verbatim = "arrives next tick") / `244+n` Expected, which also resolves the old "unresolved fifth family" at `240+n` in save_format_map.md. |

| 528 | CLOSED | AI turn-top act-state clear (`FUN_521d_0a60` raw 87560) is ported for values >= 10 only: DOS's 1/2/3 arm collides with the port's SENTRY landfall latch, which overloads the same `orders` value (`src/core/ai_euro.c`). Moved the port's own first-colony landfall goto memory off `orders == SENTRY` onto a new port-only `ColonizeUnit.ai_landfall_wait` bool (`src/core/units.h`, not saved — COLNXEXT does not carry it), set alongside `orders = UNITS_ORDER_SENTRY` at all 8 AI-unload/landfall sites in `ai_euro.c` (3 direct `ai_euro_set_goto` calls, 5 via `ai_euro_unload_pax_at`) and cleared by any non-SENTRY `ai_euro_set_goto` call. Since each golden TURN step reloads a fresh `.SAV` and the flag is not saved, col1 import (`col1_bridge.c`) now reconstructs it as `orders == SENTRY` at load time — a safe over-approximation matching this bug's own premise. `make test` 67/67, `golden_ai_turns` 6/6, `make golden` clean. |

| 544 | CLOSED | Pioneer that stepped onto a ship from land, and was then carried by that ship into a colony on the same turn, still had moves available. It should not. | FIXED 2026-09-21: DOS-checked. Walking aboard from open shore sets the passenger's spent byte +0x3149 to max (465b_05ca, #423), and nothing on the ship-move or dock path rewrites a passenger's +0x3149 (its only writers are the new-turn reset `FUN_130d_0290`, spawns and AI arms), so in DOS the unit lands in the colony spent. The port refilled the allotment on docking: `units_disembark_all` and the dock arm of `units_unload_passenger_w` treated every zero as a boarding park. Aligned fully to DOS the same day: a passenger now carries its own allotment in the hold (`ColonizeUnit.aboard_moves`, captured at boarding, reset at the day top), and waking, docking and unloading hand back exactly that. A shore crossing carries 0; boarding from a colony tile charges the water step like 465b's ADD; an overnight Sentry/Fortified park carries the full allotment. The Col1 export writes the carried spent byte for passengers instead of a flat 0. Regression in `test_units` (dock-spent: spent lands 0, partly spent lands its remainder). |
| 545 | CLOSED | Moving next to a previously uncontacted European power (particularly if encountered none before) should have an effect. IIRC DOS opens up initial diplomacy right there. | FIXED 2026-09-21: the first-contact audience (FUN_5bfb_153e: woodcut 10 + @HELLOFIRST/@HELLOAHOY, then the talk; met bit 0x20 set both ways on return, per FUN_5bfb_3180 raw 98457-98713) was already ported, but the human-move trigger only looked at adjacent units, skipped ships entirely and ignored whether tiles were land. It now follows DOS: the neighbour's owner is the unit's nation, or else the settlement owner (FUN_281f_07e0 / 06be), so an ungarrisoned foreign colony also triggers first contact. The mover's tile and the neighbour tile must both be land (FUN_281f_0768), so a ship in a colony qualifies and one at sea does not; the AI-move trigger got the same land checks. The greeting tokens replace invented English literals with DOS's raw 97696-97704 values: difficulty title + leader name, the target's country name, and @GREATKINGS / @GREATDEEDS for the target. AI-vs-AI contact still runs 13b0 from the port's periodic balance loop rather than DOS's 3180 step scan (left for golden stability). |
| 546 | CLOSED | European trade line in the top bar is broken (probably missing strings). | FIXED 2026-09-21: the sale line ("100 furs sold for 300. 0% Tax: 0. Net: 300") takes its joining words from LABELS.TXT @CMESSAGE, but `europe_set_labels` / `europe_set_messages` ran at LABELS.TXT load time, before `europe_load`, whose `memset` wiped both handles. The typed fallbacks hid this until ee6a4b5 removed them. Both are now bound right after `europe_load` in `game_loop.c`. |
| 547 | CLOSED | Indians offered to teach a servant to be a "master colonist". This is not a valid specialty. It now names every id from its NAMES.TXT @JOB row (empty on a miss). The @LEARNMASTER helper `ai_contact_learner_skill_name` had the same gap and got the same fix. |
| 548 | CLOSED | Captured colonies have broken current projects. | FIXED 2026-09-21: the captured colony carries the AI's "no project" byte 0xFF, and DOS keeps it on capture too (`FUN_5fef_1b0e` never writes +0x94; the AI tick `FUN_5952_035e` resets it to 0xFF every turn). The port drew that state wrong: a typed "-" title and a BUY button that only said "No project". DOS `FUN_2f2b_1e46` leaves the title empty, `FUN_2f2b_21da` draws BUY only when +0x94 >= 0 (CHANGE always), and `FUN_2f2b_5fc6` hit-tests BUY under the same gate. The port now does the same, so the player picks a project with CHANGE or a pane click. |
| 549 | CLOSED | 11000 gold from burning a non-capital Arawak village seems over the top, even with Cortes. | FIXED 2026-09-21: `units_conquest_treasure_gold` banded the `FUN_5fef_31ea` peel on the game difficulty, but DOS switches on `*(char *)(*(int *)0x8d4e + 2)` — the razed tribe's Indian record `tech` (Arawak = 1 Agrarian), bound by `FUN_281f_0a42` just above (raw ~101407). An Arawak village now pays 300-800, ×1.5 with Cortes (max 1200); only Aztec/Inca (tech 2/3) reach thousands. The @LOOT/@LOOT2 %STRING1/%STRING2 were typed "native"/"village"; now the tribe name and NAMES.TXT @LEVELS column 1 by tech (row 4 "Capital" for a capital), as DOS reads DS:0x9634. |
| 550 | CLOSED | Destroyed villages seem to leave behind roads. I think they didn't do that in DOS. | FIXED 2026-09-21: DOS-checked. `FUN_4d56_00e0` starts with `FUN_281f_068c(x, y, 2, 0)` (raw 81307 → `FUN_137f_015e`), which clears only the settlement bit 0x02 on the tile; a real road (0x08) stays. The port draws a road on any settlement tile, and `col1_destroy_tribe_at` never cleared the bit, so the razed site kept its road until the next save/load. It now clears it. The campaign4 saves show no road bit under the razed villages. |
| 551 | CLOSED | Destroying an Indian settlement with a mission of your country that hasn't been burned before the village is burned needs to return the missionary used to establish the mission, respawned on the former village site. Right after the destroy, `FUN_5fef_1b0e` (raw 100667-100672) spawns a Missionary (`@UNIT` 3) for the conqueror on the village tile when the mission byte names the conqueror, with profession 0x18 (Jesuit) if the mission had the Jesuit bit. A rival nation's mission is lost. The port never did this (the Jesuit mission in campaign4 at (46,28) vanished). Ported as `units_spawn_mission_return`. The convert-join now runs after the destroy, in DOS order. |
| 552 | REFUTED | Destroying a native village also should despawn any braves associated with that village that are on the map. | REFUTED 2026-09-21: the port already does what DOS does. `FUN_4d56_00e0` (raw 81310-81319) deletes every Indian unit whose home village `+0x314a` is the razed one, wherever it stands, and shifts the higher home indexes down; `col1_destroy_tribe_at` has done the same since 2026-09-07. The campaign4 saves agree: after the razes in COLONY08→09 and 09→01, no brave is still bound to a razed village. Each village has only about one bound brave, so braves still nearby belong to the tribe's other villages, and DOS keeps those too. A regression test now covers it. |
| 553 | CLOSED | Sailing a ship into a captured colony is impossible. It should behave like any other colony you own. | FIXED 2026-09-21: capture was not the cause. A native Brave had walked into Jamestown and stood there fortified (campaign4 COLONY09/01), and the ship probe bounced the owner's ship off that foreign land unit (BLOCKED_DOMAIN). DOS `FUN_465b_0000` never lands a Brave on a foreign tile: when the destination's stack-head nation, or failing that its settlement owner (`FUN_281f_07e0` / `06be`, raw 75467-75479), is foreign, it exhausts the unit (< 3 MP, `LAB_465b_01ce`) or attacks through `FUN_5fef_1b0e` (raw 75692). `ai_native_brave_step` used to commit the 021a pick as a plain step. It now stops the Brave in place and exhausts it (the attack stays parked). `units_enter_probe_w` now docks a ship in its own colony even when foreign units are standing there, which fixes the existing saves. Land units entering that colony still meet the Brave until it walks off. |
